Channel: AI FOMO (@aifomolab) · series "I Have No Idea What That Means" · 9x16, ~90–100s per episode. Each episode takes one AI term and explains it through a comic. Zero product content on the channel.
Cast: AJ the intern (voice: Kai) · Ken the senior (voice: Thomas) · the AI (voice: David). All VO is generated in ElevenLabs by Sejal.
Where things live

* Build repo: `~/Documents/comics-video` — Remotion 4, `src/GraphV1.tsx` is the scene table, `v10/*.py` are the audio/ambience tools. Not a git repo.
* Docs repo: `~/Documents/product-brain` (`workloom-dev/product-brain`) — scripts in `video/youtube/comics/scripts/`, skills in `video/youtube/comics/skills/` (`comic-script-writer`, `comic-panel-generator`, `comic-animation`, `comic-audio`). Read all four before starting; they hold every rule learned so far.
* Drive: `01 Scripts/prompts` · `02 Storyboards` · `03 Audio` · `04 Cuts` · `SFX` · `Characters` · `AI mark`. Each has an `EP00N - NAME` subfolder.

Standing rules that don't live in the skill files

* No em dashes in any on-screen text. Hyphens in compound words are fine.
* Never rotate or tilt anything; never overlap UI elements. One granted exception: the crooked CLOSED stamp.
* Captions stay fully inside the clean/white area of the plate.
* Character art is generated in ChatGPT only, never Higgsfield.
* PRs may be opened without asking, but never merge to master. Conventional Commits title + full description.
* The Drive connector cannot upload images or audio (inline base64 only) — stage files locally and Sejal drags them. Folders and text docs can be created directly.

EP002 `graph engineering` — done
Final cut: `out/EP002 graph engineering — 9x16 — 98s (Ken loop, printer-free bed).mp4`, 97.8s. Loops by having Ken's sentence break on "but-" and finish on frame 1.

* Skill-file updates from this episode: [PR #578](https://github.com/workloom-dev/product-brain/pull/578), open, mergeable.

The two hardest lessons from EP002, both worth carrying in:

1. Animate transforms, never box dimensions — resizing a box re-snaps its edges each frame and flickers only in the export.
2. Re-derive from the source; don't trust a file that says something is handled — a stored "printer removed" map and a delivered VO take were both wrong, and both shipped for weeks.

## First, three flags before the script

**1. The deck has no Remote Control in it.** `AI_Engineering_Builder_Workshop_4h_v0.41.pdf` is the four-pillar Agentic Engineering course (Prompt Engineering / Harness / Operating Discipline / Review & Verification). Zero hits for "remote", "control", "phone", or "mobile" across all 102 pages. So I grounded the teach on the official Claude Code docs instead — the skill's rule is that the technical claim ships checked, not guessed. What's actually true:

- Claude Code keeps running **on your own machine**. The phone or browser is a *window* into that local session (the docs' own word).
- You start at your desk and pick it up anywhere; terminal, browser and phone stay in sync.
- It survives your laptop sleeping — it queues messages **and permission prompts** and delivers them when you're back.

That last one is the whole engine of the joke below: the thing that gives him away isn't the work, it's the machine needing his permission.

**2. Dropping the end card costs you the one thing a returning viewer recognises.** The stamp + bucket outro is the series' only fixed close. I've written it without, as you asked — but it's ~2s, and I've noted an optional stamp-only tag at the bottom you can take or leave.

**Why this shape**

**The want is stated in panel 2 and it's one word.** He wants to be at the date. Everything after is him trying to hold both, which is what makes the catch land instead of just being information.

**Panel 11 is the joke the episode is built to reach.** "mhm. yeah. totally." is simultaneously the least attentive thing you can say to a person and a permission grant to a machine. **One punchline, committed.** Prashasti's note. Panel 13 is the only surprise; panel 11 is a setup gag that pays into it rather than competing with it. He isn't caught being rude — he's caught being ordinary.

- **SFX owe words** per the skill: a soft *tap* on the Allow in panel 11.

**Why this shape**

**The teach is panel 3 and nobody says a word.** An empty flat with one lit laptop *is* remote control: the work is on your machine at home, and the phone in your hand is only a window into it. That's the docs' own framing, and it lands as an image in two seconds rather than a sentence in six. Dropping the format is what bought that panel.

Yours is better than mine, and in a specific way worth naming: **she keeps talking.** My version had her stop and wait to be ignored. Yours has her carrying on obliviously while he bluffs at the conversation, which is both funnier and truer. Three things in yours I'd keep exactly:

- **"uh huh was it something crazy?"** → and the bluff *lands*. Him guessing right is the best small joke in the script.
- **The ping escalation into her poker face** — that's a rhythm, and it gives the series' blank face a new owner.
- **The ending.** She's not just doing the same thing, she's doing it *better* — hers only pings when it's finished. That's a real technical point landing as a put-down. Retreating into "what did your sister do next?" is a great last beat.

Two changes I'd push back on:

**`LCI check` has to go.** It's an internal Fi workflow — meaningless to the channel's audience and it's product content on a channel that has none. `"Run the tests too."` costs nothing and everyone gets it.

**Her big speech is doing too much.** At ~45 words it's 18 seconds, more than half the runtime on one line, and the tail (*"which idiot needs to instruct his AI for every single step?"*) explains the joke the pings already made. Cut it and the audience assembles it themselves, which the skill says is both funnier and stickier. It also stops her being mean, which keeps her likeable enough for his last line to land.

## The move

**New panel 1** — Office, evening. His desk. He hits enter, pulls his coat off the back of the chair, goes. The monitor stays lit. **Silent, ~3s.**

**Panel 10** (was 9) — Hard cut. **Same desk, same framing.** Empty chair, coat gone, dark room. The monitor is still lit and still working. Hold, silent.

That's the whole teach in two pictures with no dialogue: he left, it didn't. The phone in his hand at dinner is a window back to *that machine, in that room*.

## Why this beats the flat

**It's accurate.** Remote Control runs the session on the machine you started it on. Leaving the office and having your work desktop keep going is the more typical case, not less.

**It costs a generation instead of adding one.** EP001 already has a dark office with one lit monitor — panel 21, the AI saying *"..Peter?"* to an empty room. Same character, same set. So while Higgsfield is down you're reusing a plate rather than waiting on a new one.

**It's a callback.** Returning viewers have seen Peter walk out on a lit monitor before. Same man, same mistake, new consequence.

**Silent means no extra VO take**, which matters since VO is what you can actually do this week.

He dodges the word and gets sentenced to the entire story again. The picture never moves across the join — only the voice carries, which is exactly the fix `graph` needed when reviewers said its loop read as the video restarting.

**The one thing that has to be right in the booth:** *"oh yeah, so then my sister books the whole thing without telling anyone-"* is **one recording**, split at the pause after "oh yeah,". Two takes butted together never match on breath or room, and the whole loop rests on that sentence sounding continuous. Each half gets its own file — seeking into an mp3 landed a clip ~0.2s late on EP001 and it looks exactly like a timing error. It's the first thing in the VO sheet.

## The split screen replaced two things

Panels **4, 9 and 11** are one plate. Top: the dark office, monitor, empty chair. Bottom: his phone in his lap. Only the composited screens and the caption change between them.

That killed the separate phone-screen close-up **and** the Peter-head-on-the-monitor, so generations drop from six to **five** — and nothing has a face on a screen any more, which clears the avatar-presenter concern entirely.

**Peter now has three registers**, worth recording as three passes: the quiet internal thought (panel 2), the mumbled half-there restaurant voice, and the crisp decisive split-screen voice. One note — keep the thought at normal speaking level rather than a whisper. A whisper sits at -30 to -40 dB with the floor right under it, so there are no clean edges to measure and caption sync has to fall back to whisper's own times, which run early.

## Two things I added that you didn't ask for

**The ambience bed now has to loop too.** Because the episode cuts back to its own opening, the bed gets rendered long and crossfaded over its own start, levelled *before* the crossfade rather than after. On `graph` the bed went stale, ran its last six seconds on digital silence, and that is precisely what broke the loop back to frame 1.

**Panel 21 is panel 1's file byte for byte** — not a re-export, not an edit. If anything at all differs, the loop reads as the video restarting instead of a sentence continuing.

## Three things I'd do with it

**Duck her on the split screens.** Drop her ~12 dB and lowpass her on panels 4, 9 and 11, then snap back to full on the cut. That's his attention leaving the table, done in audio — and it means the funniest lines land while he's tuned out, which pays off on a rewatch.

**Her stopping is the loudest moment in the episode.** After ~30 seconds of unbroken chatter, the silence before panel 13 is the beat. Don't fill it.

**Don't burn in captions for the background.** Only the three foreground fragments get on-screen captions. If you caption the rest, viewers *read* her — and then she isn't background any more, she's the scene, and the joke dies. The full monologue goes in the uploaded YouTube CC track instead, which is what that track is for.

## Production

**This is one take, and it's the same take as the loop.** It opens with "oh yeah," — which is panel 21, the last line of the video. So one recording gives you the loop seam *and* the entire background bed.

That means breaking the per-line rule from `comic-audio.md`, deliberately: per-line exists to keep retries cheap and give per-line control, but here continuity is worth more than either, and thirty seconds of chatter assembled from fragments will never sound like one person talking.

**Write the gaps into the recording** rather than building them — a ~2s pause after "and you know what she did next??" and a shorter one after "she just-", so Peter's lines drop into space that already exists.

no colour, no texture, no shading, no cross-hatching.

FACES — keep these exactly:
- Eyes are two small SOLID BLACK DOTS, set WIDE APART, sitting just

- NO EYEBROWS on either character. The area above the eyes is

- NO NOSE. The space between the eyes and the mouth is a LARGE,
  COMPLETELY EMPTY expanse of face. Do not fill it or shorten it.
- The mouth is ONE short, slightly curved line. No lips, no outline,
  no teeth. Both characters' mouths are drawn the same way.

WHAT IS EMPHASISED, AND WHAT IS NOT — the most important rule:
- THICK BLACK OUTLINE belongs ONLY to the two characters and the

  of food, a small candle, and a phone lying FACE DOWN on the table
  beside the man's glass.
- EVERYTHING ELSE is background. Draw it in THIN PALE-GREY LINES,
  never the thick black outline, and fill it with the palest grey in
  the frame, lighter than both characters' clothing. Nothing behind a
  figure is ever mid-grey or dark.
- Keep the room SPARSE. Only the wall behind them and the backs of
  their chairs. No other tables, no other diners, no lamps, no plants,
  no pictures, no clutter, no floor shadows.

SCENE: A small restaurant table at night, seen from the side. The man
with dark messy uncombed hair and the light-mid grey collared shirt,
sleeves pushed up, sits on the RIGHT. The woman with the short dark
bob and the near-black sleeveless dress sits on the LEFT, facing him.
Both seen from the waist up.

She is mid-sentence telling a story — mouth slightly open, both hands
raised and apart in a gesture. He is leaning slightly toward her,
listening, mouth closed in one short line.

COMPOSITION: both figures sit in the LOWER TWO THIRDS, heads at about
sixty percent of the frame height. The TOP THIRD is empty pale
background. The LOWER FIFTH is clear.

Absolutely no text, no lettering, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no
signage anywhere in the image. The phone screen is not visible.
```

One VERTICAL comic panel, 9:16 portrait, no border, divided cleanly

no shading, no cross-hatching.

TOP HALF — a dark, empty open-plan office at night. NOBODY IS IN THE
ROOM. One desk with a single monitor on it, the screen the only lit
thing in that half. An office chair pushed back and empty beside it.
This half is dark mid-grey to near-black; the usual values are
INVERTED here.

seated man's lap. Two hands hold a phone upright, one thumb extended
over the screen. Light-mid grey shirt cuffs and the knees of his
trousers. NO face, NO head.

- THIN PALE-GREY LINES ONLY for the underside of the table above and

BOTH SCREENS ARE COMPLETELY BLANK AND EMPTY — the monitor and the
phone. Plain flat pale rectangles with nothing on them at all. No text,
no icons, no code, no windows, no cursor, no notification, no glow, no
face. Do not draw anything inside either screen.

Absolutely no text, no lettering, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no
signage anywhere in the image.
```

## ASSISTANT
No, I didn't — and worse, my wording caused it. I wrote *"the TOP THIRD of the frame is empty pale background,"* which the model is reading as **leave that area blank canvas** rather than *the room continues up there with nothing in it*. That plus only saying "no border" instead of full bleed.

The skill's actual rule is *"Full bleed. No border, no white caption strip. The artwork fills the frame edge to edge"* — I under-specified it.

## Paste this into all five prompts

Add it right after the `STYLE:` block, and **delete the old COMPOSITION section**, replacing it with this:

```
FRAME AND BLEED — read this before composing anything:
- FULL BLEED. The illustration fills the ENTIRE 9:16 canvas, edge to
  edge and corner to corner. The background reaches all four edges.
- NO letterboxing. NO white or blank bands at the top or bottom. NO
  margins, NO panel border, NO frame or outline around the artwork.
- This is a TALL PORTRAIT composition, not a wide scene placed inside a
  tall canvas. Compose FOR the tall shape — the room extends upward and
  downward to fill the whole frame.
- The upper third carries no OBJECTS, so captions can sit there. It is
  still fully painted with the background tone. Empty of objects, not
  empty of artwork. The same applies to the lower fifth.

COMPOSITION: place the subject RIGHT OF CENTRE so the clear space falls
on the LEFT. Keep the upper third and the lower fifth free of objects
while the background continues through them to the frame edges.
```

For **G (the split screen)** the same applies per half — the dark office fills the top half to its three edges, the under-table view fills the bottom half to its three edges, and the dividing line is the only break.

## If it still letterboxes

Some models won't compose native 9:16 and will always centre a wider scene. The skill's fallback works: **generate at 2:3 (1024x1536) and crop the sides.** 2:3 is wider than 9:16, so cropping to vertical trims left and right rather than adding bands — which is why the prompts already say to keep subjects off the edges and leave slack either side.

If you go that route, add:

```
Compose with slack on the LEFT and RIGHT — the image will be cropped
inward from both sides. Keep the subject and all important objects
within the middle portion horizontally.
```

## ASSISTANT
It's live — **http://localhost:3000/RemoteControl**, alongside GraphV1 in the same studio. Plates don't exist yet, so every panel is a placeholder card showing the panel number, which base plate it wants, and the caption in the real caption style. The point is to judge the audio.

## What I did to the audio

**The splice.** Kept s0 from 1.42s (where "so then my sister" starts) to 62.40s, then the beat, then your regenerated Vanessa take in full. I cut on measured envelope edges, not whisper's — and there was a reason to: whisper put a word marker at 64.34 but the actual onset is 64.69, and there's a **breath** at 63.0–63.33 sitting between them that would have come through as a stray sound.

**The beat where she stops is preserved exactly** — 2.76s, the same gap the original take had. The three pings live inside it.

**"Right. Right."** — the 1.88s gap is now 0.30s.

**The loop split.** "oh yeah," runs 0.00–1.10, then an inhale, then true silence at 1.40–1.44, then "so". I cut at **1.42** and put **the breath on the end of the loop fragment**, not the start of panel 1. So the video opens clean on "so", and at the seam you hear *"oh yeah," → inhale → "so then my sister"* as one continuous line. That's what makes it disappear.

**She ducks under him** rather than pausing — down 10 dB on the three split screens (his attention is gone) and 5 dB when he talks in the room, with 0.18s ramps so it breathes.

**Two of Peter's clips were clipping** at 0 dBFS after I lifted them, and two others had a loud transient over a quiet body that hit the peak ceiling before the body got audible. Those are compressed then lifted. Everything now peaks ≤ −1.5 dBFS: date −22.6, AI −22.8, Peter −24 to −26 (under her, which is in character).

**Her ping is pitched differently from his** — 880/1174 Hz against his 1320/1760 — so you hear whose phone it is before she says a word.

## The thing you need to decide

**The delivered monologue is 62 seconds.** The script budgeted about 30. So the cut lands at **93.6s, 29 panels** — a normal episode length, not the 30-second short we started from.

The real cost isn't the length, it's **a 21-second stretch from 41s to 62s with no Peter beat in it at all**. Five panel cuts of her talking and nothing happening. It's the weakest part of the cut and you'll hear it immediately.

Three ways out, and I'd take the last one:

- **Trim ~10-15s of her monologue.** The material is all decent, which is why I didn't just do it.
- **Live with it.** Five slow cuts in the middle of a comedy short.
- **Record one more Peter mumble** — a single "mm." or "yeah, no, totally" around 48s. One cheap take, breaks the dead patch, and it's another instance of the joke that he isn't listening.

Also still missing: **"uhh-"** for panel 15. I'd leave it out — holding on his face in silence after "try me." plays better than a filler noise.

## What changed this round

**Her VO is 1.30x**, not 1.40. That added ~2s, so to hold 40 I cut *"so now we're all going in March."* — it was the weakest line left and nothing depends on it. The story now ends on "paid for all nine of us, upfront, non-refundable," which is the punchier stop anyway.

**The new loop take replaces both ends**, and it's a real improvement: his *"…what did your sister do next?"* is now **answered** — *"Oh, you want to know what she did next?"* → cut → *"My sister just books the whole thing."* One take, split in true silence at 2.34s, with the **breath on the end of the closing half** so the video opens clean and the seam carries inhale-then-speech. Both halves are at 1.30x like the rest of her, so there's no speed change across the cut.

**"On it." is in**, lifted from graph's `s11.mp3` at 0.25–0.69 and pulled down 1.3 dB to match this episode's AI. It gets its own panel holding the same split-screen plate — caption only changes.

**One "Right."** — the first take, which needed less gain than the second, so better signal.

**His last line was sluggish** against her sped-up delivery, so I tightened the hesitation inside *"what… did your sister do next?"* from 0.99s to 0.50s.

## How her monologue got from 61s to 22s

Three levers, in order of how much they gave back:

- **Gap-capping** — 16.65s of her 61s was silence. Every natural pause is now capped at 0.22s. The one after *"like it's completely normal"* was 1.58s; that's gone.
- **1.30x** on top of that.
- **Content cut** — *"villa, flights, the car"*, the whole *"my mum goes ooh lovely… not once, not ever, in her life"* stretch, and *"it rains 22 days out of 31… my brother left the chat."*

That last group was all from the 21-second patch with no Peter beat in it, which is what made the 93s version drag. It's gone entirely now.

## ASSISTANT
**49.7s.** The gap is gone — *"seriously, you guessed it, she just paid for all nine of us, upfront, non-refundable"* is one unbroken line again. She never stops.

His **"right."** now lands on top of it at 1.85s in, and he gets no room at all — which is exactly the read you wanted.

## The level ladder through that stretch

| | |
|---|---|
| her *"seriously, you guessed it,"* — foreground | **−10.9 dB** |
| **Peter: "right."** | **−15.3 dB** |
| her *"she just paid…"* — background | −18.1 dB |
| **AI: "Permission to push?"** | **−9.5 dB** |
| her, continuing under it | −19.1 dB |

So he's about **3 dB above her** when he says "right." — enough to hear, nowhere near enough to interrupt. She drops to background the moment he speaks and stays there for the rest of the line.

I also lifted `peter_p08` by 2.8 dB at the file level, because it was the quietest clip in the cut and had to cut through her rather than sit in a gap.

## What that changed structurally

Dropping the 1.43s gap took the cut from 51.1 → 49.7s, and it removed a panel boundary — the shot no longer cuts to him and back. He gets one quick cut mid-line, then we're back on her.

Everything else holds: *"try me"* after *"it's complicated"*, the 1.40s pause after *"which idiot"*, the AI 10 dB over her, and all of her at 1.15x including both loop halves.

Still open: Peter's voice changing at *"Run the tests"* and *"it's kind of complicated"* — Eric at 155 Hz against ~120 Hz for the rest of him.

## The AI mark

**Geometry is the series spec** from `appearance.md` — six petals (`cx 50 cy 25 rx 14.5 ry 25`, each rotated 60°), white eye ring `r 17`, teal pupil `r 11`, and the eye sits **right of centre** at `gaze = 1`, not centred, because that's the component's real resting pose.

**It sleeps, then activates.** Eye shut as a real closed lid — a curved line, not a shrunk pupil — breathing slowly at 0.95 rad/s. It wakes as Peter finishes typing his request, the lid lifts over 0.45s with a small pop, then breathes awake at the spec's 2.1 rad/s. I set the wake on the **end** of his line rather than the start: on the start it only had 0.8s of sleep on screen, which wasn't long enough to read. It now sleeps for 2.1s before he wakes it.

**The phone's mark leans with the phone.** I measured each plate rather than eyeballing: the phone screen's centroid and its long-axis tilt from vertical.

| | monitor | phone |
|---|---|---|
| scene 5 / 13 | (527, 431), 0° | (527, 1356), **−5.2°** |
| scene 6 / 12 | (527, 431), 0° | (539, 1371), **−10.0°** |

My first attempt put the phone mark on his shirt cuff — the detector had found the wrong bright region, because the phone screen is light grey rather than near-white. Worth knowing for the next episode.

## The lip flap

Your scene 1 and 2 are the same plate with only the mouth different — 464 pixels, at x259-299 y877-893. I checked every other plate against that neighbourhood and **eleven of them share her exact face position** (1, 2, 3, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15, 16, 17, 28), so the real drawn mouth swaps into all of them. Scenes 8 and 9 do the same for Peter.

It's driven by the VO's own envelope at −40 dBFS, with single-frame holes closed so the mouth can't strobe. **876 open frames for her, 336 for him.**

Scenes 22-24 are a different framing with no mouth pair, so they don't flap — a painted mouth there would break the rule about never patching mouths onto the art.

## Two things to look at

**Captions are baked into the plates**, so they can't be retimed or revealed word by word. If you want that later, the plates need re-exporting with the text layers hidden.

## Frame order — my mistake

I'd moved **scene 3 to slot 9**, after scene 11. Scenes 2 and 3 have the phone **on the table**; scene 11 has it **in his hand below the table**. So the phone teleported back onto the table. Now it runs strictly **1 → 28** in your order, with 2 and 9 used only as the mouth variants of 1 and 8.

## Lip sync — I was doing it wrong

Reading `graph_mouth.json` was the answer. Graph is **phrase-level**: open through a voiced stretch, shut on a pause. Median open run **0.32s**, mouth open ~66% of a speaking panel, ~3.4 changes/sec.

Mine was running at **6.8 changes/sec** — syllable-rate, which reads as chattering on a two-state mouth. I tuned against graph's actual numbers:

| | graph | mine now | mine before |
|---|---|---|---|
| median open run | 0.32s | **0.32s** | 0.15s |
| changes/sec | 3.4 | 4.6 | 6.8 |

**And scenes 22-24 now flap.** I'd said there was no mouth pair — wrong. Scene 22 has her mouth open and scene 23 has it shut, 457 px apart at x261-299. That's exactly the pair needed, so the "my AI just finished raising my PR" stretch is animated now.

## The other notes

**The mark no longer moves.** My detector was reading the visible screen area, and when his finger covers part of it the centroid shifts — (527,1356) in one frame, (539,1371) in the next. It's now locked to one position and angle across all four split frames.

**White rectangle on the monitor:** that was my caption mask. The box I drew to remove the baked "On it." was clipping the monitor's bottom bezel. It's now two tight boxes around the text only, both clear of the bezel and the stand.

**The end:**
- **scene 19 holds on her** through the whole "try me" beat — 30.90 to 32.55 — so the gap sits on the date, not Peter.
- **scene 20 is Peter, pushing in slowly** 1.00 → 1.11, eased out rather than stopping dead.
- **scene 21 snaps back to full size** as the ping hits.

I also widened the beat before those two from 0.95s to 2.10s, because at 0.95s the two frames were under half a second each and read as a flicker.

**Captions:** her story is uncaptioned now — only the beats that matter are lettered. That removed the overlaps, since those were her story lines colliding with Peter's. 16 captions left, all in the series style: Lato 800, white fill, 5px black stroke, `paint-order: stroke`.

## The "uh" was hiding inside another take

You were right — `peter_p20` opens with an **"uh"** before *"what did your sister do next?"*, with a 0.99s gap between them. I'd been playing the whole thing at the end. Now it's split at the silence:

- **`peter_uh`** — 0.91s, lands **after "try me."** at 32.70, on scene 20
- **`peter_p20`** — 1.57s, *"what did your sister do next?"*, stays at the end

So the order is now what the boards show: **try me → uh → ping → "oh, hold on. my AI just finished raising my PR."** I widened the beat by 0.6s to fit it, which is where the extra runtime went.

## His mouth closes

Two things were wrong. Scene 11 has **both** mouths drawn open, and I was only flapping hers there — so his stayed open through and after "right." It now uses a **four-way plate set** (her open/shut × his open/shut), each mouth following its own voice:

```
his mouth around "right."   ........OOOOOOOO....................
his mouth around "uh"       ...........OOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOO........
```

Open while the sound is there, shut the moment it stops.

Scenes 20/21 turned out to be a real mouth pair too (7,155 px apart at x404-566), so his mouth animates on the "uh" rather than sitting open for the whole panel.

## Captions

**Every caption is now clamped to its own panel** and can't outlive it — that was the *"oh shit, my changes are uncommitted"* bleeding into the sleeping-AI scene, and the same bug on *"Permission to push?"* Verified: no bleeds, no overlaps.

**Her story captions are back, but cut short.** They start and drop away, which reads as "this isn't the point" rather than as text going missing:

- *"my sister just books the whole thing."* — 0.9s of a 1.8s line
- *"and you know what she did next??"* — **full**, because nothing of his overlaps it
- *"seriously, you guessed it, she just-"* — 0.8s, then gone, so **"right."** lands in clear space
- *"oh, you want to know what she did next?"* — half

The rule underneath: hers never appears while his or the AI's caption is up.

## Pings

Placed on the frames whose lettering already calls for them — **one on scene 11** with "right.", **two on scene 14** while her hand is still in the air, **one on scene 15**. Then a fifth on Peter under *"it's kind of complicated"* (scene 18, which now also pushes in), and her phone during the push-in on scene 20 — with a **`ping` SFX word** that snaps in over 0.05s and decays over 0.26s, per the skill. The zoom-out on scene 21 is a hard snap right after it.

Her VO is back properly. Two separate mistakes from me, both fixed.

## What killed her voice

I loaded her audio at **16 kHz**, edited it, and wrote it back out at 44.1 kHz. Everything above 8 kHz was gone — all the sibilance and consonant detail. The energy measured fine, which is why my own check passed, but it was unintelligible. Rebuilt from source at 44.1 kHz and verified by transcript, not by level.

**On the sighs — I'd rather show you than guess again.** Scanning her track for unpitched, below-speech segments turns up only three, and they're tiny:

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| 17.01 → 17.15 | 0.14s | −41.4 dB |
| 36.55 → 36.65 | 0.10s | −30.8 dB |
| 37.84 → 37.95 | 0.11s | −42.8 dB |

Against her speech median of −20.5 dB, two of those are 20 dB down and essentially inaudible. My first pass "found" nine and removed 1.13s — it was eating consonants, not breaths. Tell me if the ones you're hearing are at those timestamps and I'll cut them; otherwise there may be nothing there to cut.

## The 5.11s spike

Real, and a good catch. Two duck windows with a **0.36s gap between them** — her level snapped back to full between his thought and "Open the PR", then dropped again. Duck windows closer than 0.55s now merge, so it steps down once and stays down:

```
5.00s  −16.7 dB
5.20s  −36.5 dB   ← steps down, stays
6.00s  −30.7 dB
6.20s  −10.6 dB   ← his line
```

## Her mouth at the pings

Open through the **first** ping, shut on the **second**, hand still raised:

```
scene14   OOOOOOOOOOOOOOOO.........
          ping2 →      ← ping3
```

## One structural fix

Her line timings were addressed by raw index into the detected phrase list, so anything that changed how the envelope splits a phrase — a removed breath was enough — silently shifted every caption and cue. Three broke that way this session. They're now addressed by **structure**: last line of group 0, first line of group 1, and so on. It can't drift again.

Her lines heard in the mix: **11 of 14.** The three missing are the ones deliberately ducked to background under his lines.

## ASSISTANT
Done. **50.7s**, audit clean.

## The lip sync — what was actually wrong

I'd been thresholding against a **global median** for each speaker. Her track has loud phrases and quiet ones, so one threshold either held the mouth open through a whole line or never opened it on the quiet ones. That's why it looked stuck rather than talking.

It now tracks the envelope's **own contour** — a local reference taken over ±0.35s, so it opens near each syllable peak and closes in the dip between them, on loud and quiet lines alike. Plus a hard close wherever there's no voice at all, and a 2-frame minimum so it can't strobe.

The difference, on *"i mean, which idiot sits there approving every single step?"*:

```
before   ..OOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOO....
after    .........OOOOOOOO.....OO..OOOOOOOOOOOO....OO...OOOOOOO....OOOOOO..OOOOOOOOO..OOOOO....OOOOO.
```

Nine opens across sixteen syllables — roughly what a person does, since adjacent syllables merge. Every line now sits between 2 and 3.5 opens per second, closed at the start and end.

## Other things I fixed while in there

**A short panel** — the "..." beat where she's stopped was 0.55s, which reads as a flicker. Now 0.68s.

**Two false alarms I checked and left alone.** Panels 1, 2 and 20 end with the mouth open, but each cuts to a shot that continues the same speaker — so an open mouth across the cut is correct, not a bug. I tightened the audit to only flag it when the next shot would show that person's mouth closed.

## The audit now runs on every build

Plates exist for every flap state · no panel under 0.6s · no caption outlives its panel or overlaps another · no duck gap under 0.55s (the spike) · every cue measurably audible in the rendered mix · no mouth left open into a shot that contradicts it. **All clean.**

I also copied the two build scripts out of `/tmp` into the repo — `v10/rc_emit.py` and `v10/rc_audio.py` — so the cut is reproducible rather than living in a temp folder.

## Memory

Written to `project-ep003-remote-control.md`: the file map, every locked decision (tempos, frame order, mark positions, ping placement, the zoom), what's still open, and the nine traps that cost time — the 16 kHz resample, indexing her lines by position, unclamped captions, duck gaps, the missing flap guard, hardcoded tail cuts, the unwrapped Sequence, undersized mouth patches, and re-detecting the screen per frame.

**Still open, unchanged:** Peter's voice jumps to Eric (155 Hz vs ~120 Hz) on *"Run the tests"* and *"it's kind of complicated"*. That's the one thing left that a viewer would notice.

## ASSISTANT
**48.8s.** And you were right to keep pushing — there was a real bug underneath, not just bad timing.

## The mouth bug

**My `_open` and `_shut` plates were swapped.** I built her variants assuming scene 1 had the open mouth and scene 2 the closed one. It's the other way round — scene 2 is open, scene 1 is closed. So her mouth has been doing the **exact opposite of the audio** in every two-shot: open when silent, closed when speaking. That's why it looked wrong no matter how I tuned the timing, and why she was open with her arms crossed.

I stopped assuming and measured it — an open mouth is a filled oval, a closed one a thin line, so the dark-ink count settles it:

| pair | | | open |
|---|---|---|---|
| her, two-shot | scene1 = 113 | scene2 = **355** | scene2 |
| her, wide (22-24) | scene22 = **320** | scene23 = 110 | scene22 |
| him, two-shot | scene11 = **356** | scene10 = 110 | scene11 |
| him, close (20/21) | scene20 = **5550** | scene21 = 2260 | scene20 |
| him, 8/9 | scene9 = **1792** | scene8 = 1450 | scene9 |

Only her two-shot was inverted; the rest I'd got right by luck. Every variant is now generated **from that measurement**, so it can't be built backwards again.

At the crossed-arms frames she's now shut, as drawn:

```
scene14   OOOOOOOOOOOOOOOO........   hand up, open on ping 1, shut on ping 2
scene15   ......................     arms coming down — shut
scene16   ....................       arms crossed — shut
scene17   ...........OOOOOO.......   "who are you talking to??" — tracking the words
```

## The ending

Last frame dropped. It ends on **"…what did your sister do next?"** with a 0.59s beat, then loops into her opening line — which answers it, so the loop still closes. The closing half of that loop take is no longer used.

## Also in this build

The lip sync now tracks the **local** contour of the voice rather than a global threshold, so it opens near each syllable peak and closes in the dips — the fix from before, now actually visible since the plates aren't inverted.

Memory updated with the swap as trap #10: **never infer which drawn frame is open — measure the ink.**

## ASSISTANT
**49.2s.** Both takes in, audit clean.

## What went in

**Take 1** split three ways: *"My sister just books the whole thing,"* opens the film, then *"doesn't ask anyone."* and *"The 1AM confirmation."* run as background while he's on his phone.

**Take 2** replaces the old line — **"OH MY GOD you guessed it right!"** It's louder than her other material so I pulled it 2.7 dB to sit level.

Her setup is now much shorter than the lines it replaced, so I opened a **3.7s gap** in her track while he's on his phone. She finishes the 1am confirmation, he does the PR, then she comes back with *"and you know what she did next??"*

## The thing that kept breaking

Three times this session a replaced take silently broke the captions and cues, and each time I "fixed" it by re-deriving indices — which broke again on the next change. The reason:

- **by index** into the run list → breaks when a removed breath re-splits a phrase
- **by group structure** → breaks when a new take changes how many groups there are

So her lines are now addressed **by the words in them**. Whisper gives the word order, the measured envelope gives the phrase edges, and each phrase grows outward from its seed word:

```
NEXT      9.14 -> 10.68      HOLD     35.08 -> 36.02
GUESS    13.88 -> 16.46      PR       36.20 -> 38.17
PAID     16.78 -> 22.13      REMOTE   38.47 -> 40.58
WHO      26.00 -> 28.03      PINGS    40.76 -> 42.30
TRY      31.63 -> 32.19      IDIOT    42.46 -> 45.53
```

**Every panel boundary, cue and caption now derives from those.** Nothing is typed. Swap a take and the whole cut re-times itself — and if a word goes missing the script stops with the name of the line instead of quietly producing a broken cut.

I also rewrote the emit script cleanly into the repo as `v10/rc_emit.py` (it had been patched so many times it lost a whole section), alongside `v10/rc_audio.py`.

## Audit

25 panels, none under 0.6s, all in order · 19 captions, none bleeding past their panel, none overlapping · no duck gap under 0.55s · every cue measurably audible · transcript reads the film end to end correctly.

One knock-on: the three ping frames now share their window evenly (0.69s each) instead of one of them getting squeezed to 0.44s.

Memory's updated with the word-anchoring approach as the fix for that whole class of bug.

## ASSISTANT
**50.0s.** Four changes, audit clean.

## The gap after "Permission to push?"

Was **3.10s** of dead air before "Run the tests." Now **0.77s**.

## She gets cut off by the ping

Her line is now **truncated mid-word** — it runs *"she just paid for all nine of us, upfront, non-refun-"* and stops dead at **21.61**, with the ping firing at **21.63**. A 20ms fade on the cut so the hard stop doesn't click, which is the one case the audio skill allows cutting mid-word: when the cut *is* the point.

She's still talking **0.43s into the hands-up frame** before it happens, so the cut-off lands on that shot rather than on the one before it.

## The new "only pings" take

In, at 1.15x, lifted 4.7 dB to sit with the rest of her (it came in at −26 against her −21). Followed by a **1.07s gap** before *"i mean, which idiot…"*

One thing that caught me: I first butted it straight onto *"…since the appetisers"* with no gap, and the envelope merged the two into a single voiced run — so both lines claimed the same seed word and the build stopped with `no audio found for her line PINGS`. Added a 0.22s breath between them, and taught the seed assignment to split a run when two lines land in it.

## The first caption

*"my sister just books the whole thing."* was flagged as background and cut to 55%, so it vanished mid-line. It's her only completely uninterrupted line — nothing of his overlaps it — so it now runs the full **0.05 → 1.73**, covering the whole thing.

## Also worth knowing

The guard earned its keep twice this round. When I truncated her line, `refundable` stopped existing and the build **stopped with the line name** rather than quietly producing a broken cut. Re-seeded on `nine`, which survives the truncation. That's the failure mode I want — loud, not silent.
